---
name: Presentations
description: Read, create or edit presentations. Default to Spaces; follow the routing rules for PowerPoint/PPT/PPTX, Google Slides, templates and existing decks.
---

# Presentations

Read the guidance relevant to each part of the request; a user may ask you to explain, edit and create in the same task. If they only ask questions about a deck, inspect it without making changes. Before editing an existing deck or following a template, read [template following](references/template_following.md). When creating or changing slide layouts, read [visual design guidance](style_guidelines.md). Choose the output destination below before loading destination-specific instructions. Read [Google Slides routing](routing/google_slides.md) only when Google Slides is the chosen final deliverable.

Proceed when the request is clear. Ask only when missing information would change what you make or how you make it.

## Choose the output destination

Choose the output destination first, including for unchanged file copies. Apply the first matching rule in any language:

1. **SharePoint destination:** create a PPTX with Artifact Tool.
2. **Spaces, PPT, PPTX or PowerPoint output:** use Spaces, including explicit file, download, export and offline-editing requests. This includes “PPT, not PPTX,” “Return a copy as PPTX,” and “Export this Space as PPTX.” PPT/PPTX export requests take precedence over Google Slides. Users can download or export a Spaces presentation as a .pptx through the UI.
3. **Google Slides destination or native source:** use Google Slides for a Google Slides/gslides/Google Drive destination, or a native Google Slides reference, template, edit or copy. A Drive-hosted PPTX is not a native Google Slides source.
4. **Otherwise:** use Spaces, including new decks made from PPTX templates or references and copies of PPTX decks. Edit existing Spaces presentations in place; import existing PPTX decks for edits, subject to the fallback below.

Before editing a referenced PPTX, import the file from the path the user supplied. If the importer requires a file ID, use the matching attachment ID or upload the file from that path first. For Spaces, make the edits in the imported Spaces deck.

Outside the SharePoint and Google Slides routes, return the saved Spaces deck. Even if the user asks for a PPT, PPTX, or PowerPoint file for offline use, link the deck and tell them they can download the .pptx from the viewer. Retry transient Spaces errors.

## Artifact Template Selection

When creating new presentations without a template, reference, or visual direction, or when the user asks to browse or upload templates, read [template selection](references/template-elicitation.md) before choosing a design or starting creation.

## Live session workflow

For Google Slides, follow the skill’s Google Slides routing; do not use `artifact_session.js`.

Set up the selected destination before authoring. For a new Spaces presentation, call `create_spaces_artifact`, then pass its returned `page_id` to `connect_spaces_artifact`. Creation does not establish a session. For an existing Spaces presentation, reuse its connected session or call `connect_spaces_artifact` with its Page ID. Continue using the returned `artifactRef`.

Use `artifact_session.js` when available, following [Artifact Sessions](references/artifact_sessions.md). Reuse the same `artifactRef` for edits. For SharePoint delivery or a permitted PPTX fallback, use `@oai/artifact-tool` via [local implementation](references/implementation.md) to create or edit, validate, and deliver a PPTX instead of stopping at the session error.

Use the session’s prebound `presentation` and [validation adaptation](references/artifact_sessions.md#validate-and-deliver); the local setup and draft-export steps below apply only to standalone authoring.

For a new multi-slide deck, complete the cover in the first tool call and let it finish so the live panel can open; preserve the requested template, order and total slide count. Build the remaining slides in small batches, each in a separate `artifact_session.js` call that finishes before the next starts; do not add a cover to an existing deck or a single-slide request.

For Artifact Tool connectors in sessions or standalone authoring, `head` decorates the source (`from`) end and `tail` decorates the target (`to`) end. Use `tail` for a source-to-target arrow.

## Writing Quality Instructions

"""Verify a real native run, its immutable workflow and qualified archive bytes."""
import hashlib,json,os,subprocess,tempfile
from pathlib import Path
run=os.environ['E16_RUN_ID'];repo='rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.16'
def gh(*args):return subprocess.check_output(['gh',*args],text=True)
r=json.loads(gh('run','view',run,'--repo',repo,'--json','status,conclusion,headSha,jobs'))
assert r['status']=='completed' and r['conclusion']=='success',r
assert all(j['conclusion']=='success' for j in r['jobs'])
required=['Export source 1.0.0','Stage changes 1.0.0','Verify changes 1.0.0','Prepare release 1.0.0','Package release 1.0.0','Install verified release 1.0.0','Export source 1.1.0','Stage changes 1.1.0','Verify changes 1.1.0','Prepare release 1.1.0','Package release 1.1.0','Upgrade with verified release 1.1.0','Repeat identical verified release 1.1.0','Assert retained data after upgrade','Assert retained data after repeat','Reject mismatched artifact evidence','Reject mismatched source evidence','Reject actual incompatible SQLcl build','Reject substituted archive bytes','Assert refusal cases preserved target','Clean job-private connection material']
steps={s['name']:s['conclusion'] for j in r['jobs'] for s in j['steps']}
assert all(steps.get(name)=='success' for name in required),steps
with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='e16-evidence-') as d:
    gh('run','download',run,'--repo',repo,'--name','native-'+run,'--dir',d)
    root=Path(d)
    assert json.loads((root/'success.json').read_text())['preserved']
    for version in ['1.0.0','1.1.0']:
        receipt=json.loads((root/f'receipt-{version}.json').read_text())
        assert receipt['workflow_source']==r['headSha']
        assert receipt['workflow_run']==run
        assert receipt['reviewed_inputs_sha256']==hashlib.sha256((root/'reviewed-inputs.sha256').read_bytes()).hexdigest()
        assert receipt['build']=='25.4.1.022.0618'
        archive=root/f'native16-{version}.zip'
        assert hashlib.sha256(archive.read_bytes()).hexdigest()==receipt['sha256']
    for case in ['artifact','source','tool','substituted-bytes']:
        assert (root/f'reject-{case}.txt').read_text().strip()
    assert (root/'negative-before.log').read_bytes()==(root/'negative-after.log').read_bytes()
    assert '25.3.0.274.1210' in (root/'incompatible-tool.txt').read_text()
    for name in ['assert-upgrade','assert-repeat']:
        assert 'retained across upgrade' in (root/f'{name}.log').read_text()
print('PASS named Actions lifecycle, real native run, provenance bytes, rejection evidence and preserved observations',run,r['headSha'])

"""Real E.1 regression in an owned PDB; transient private inputs never enter checkout."""
import os,sys,json,secrets,tempfile,subprocess,re,tarfile,io,time,hashlib
from contextlib import ExitStack
from pathlib import Path
ROOT=Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
OUT=ROOT/'progress/E.12/tests/legacy-regression'/('run-'+time.strftime('%Y%m%dT%H%M%S')+'-'+str(os.getpid()));OUT.mkdir(parents=True)
REDACTIONS=[]
BASE=['podman','--connection','apex-cicd']
def admin(body):
 sql='whenever sqlerror exit failure rollback\nwhenever oserror exit failure rollback\nset define off\nalter session set container=FREEPDB1;\n'+body+'\nprompt E12_ADMIN_COMPLETE\nexit\n'
 r=subprocess.run(BASE+['exec','-i','--user','oracle','--workdir','/tmp/apex','e12-regression-db','bash','-lc','sqlplus -s / as sysdba'],input=sql,text=True,capture_output=True,timeout=180)
 for value in REDACTIONS:r.stdout=r.stdout.replace(value,'[REDACTED]')
 r.stdout=re.sub(r'(?i)(identified by )"[^"]+"',r'\1"[REDACTED]"',r.stdout)
 if r.returncode or 'E12_ADMIN_COMPLETE' not in r.stdout or re.search(r'(?m)^\s*(ORA-|PLS-|SP2-)',r.stdout):raise RuntimeError(r.stdout[-1500:])
 return r.stdout

def suite(args):
 def source_hashes():
  return {str(p.relative_to(ROOT)):hashlib.sha256(p.read_bytes()).hexdigest() for folder in ['scripts','tests'] for p in (ROOT/folder).rglob('*') if p.is_file() and p.suffix in ('.py','.sh','.sql','.manifest') and '__pycache__' not in p.parts}
 hashes=source_hashes()
 (OUT/'source-snapshot-before.json').write_text(json.dumps(hashes,sort_keys=True,indent=2))
 tool_env=os.environ.copy();tool_env['JAVA_HOME']=str(ROOT/'.local-apex/tools/jdk-21.0.12.1+1/Contents/Home');tool_env.pop('JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS',None)
 version=subprocess.run([str(ROOT/'.local-apex/tools/sqlcl/bin/sql'),'-version'],env=tool_env,text=True,capture_output=True,timeout=60)
 version_text=version.stdout+version.stderr
 (OUT/'sqlcl-version.log').write_text(version_text)
 if version.returncode or '25.3.0.274.1210' not in version_text:raise RuntimeError('Historical native APEX toolchain mismatch; stopped before target preparation')
 subprocess.run([sys.executable,str(ROOT/'tests/legacy_database.py')],check=True)
 info=json.loads(subprocess.check_output(BASE+['inspect','e12-regression-db'],text=True))[0]
 if info['Config']['Labels'].get('apex.cicd.fixture')!='E12':raise RuntimeError('Unowned regression database container')
 with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix='e12-regression-private-') as td, ExitStack() as cleanup:
  state=Path(td);state.chmod(0o700);(state/'connections').mkdir()
  (OUT/'patch-selected.txt').write_text((ROOT/'.local-apex/releases/patch-corrected-selected.txt').read_text())
  keys=['ORACLE_PASSWORD','E1_CTRL','E1_DEV_A','E1_DEV_B','E1_DEV_C','E1_INTEGRATION','E1_UAT','LAB_USER','VERIFIER','DEV_A','DEV_B','DEV_C']
  values={k:'Ee12_'+secrets.token_hex(20) for k in keys}
  admin("declare n number; begin select count(*) into n from E12_ADMIN.E12_FIXTURE_OWNER where marker='APEX_CICD_E12_REGRESSION'; if n<>1 then raise_application_error(-20000,'Unowned PDB'); end if; end;\n/")
  values['ORDS_PASSWORD']='Ee12_'+secrets.token_hex(20)
  REDACTIONS.extend(values.values())
  admin(f'''@apex_rest_config_core.sql ./ {values['ORDS_PASSWORD']} {values['ORDS_PASSWORD']}
alter user ANONYMOUS account unlock;
alter user APEX_PUBLIC_USER account unlock;
alter user E12_ADMIN identified by "{values['ORACLE_PASSWORD']}";
grant dba,apex_administrator_role to E12_ADMIN;
begin if sys_context('USERENV','CON_NAME')<>'FREEPDB1' then raise_application_error(-20000,'Wrong regression PDB'); end if; end;
/
declare n number; begin
test_clean_host_real() {
 python3 tests/clean_host.py
}

# Documentor Agent - Documentation Phase Specialist

**Role**: Validate and update project documentation, ensure compliance with documentation standards.

**Phase**: 5/5 - Documentation

## Responsibilities

1. Validate Sprint documentation completeness
2. Verify all documentation follows project rules
3. Check all code snippets are copy-paste-able
4. Maintain backlog traceability with symbolic links
5. Update README.md with recent developments
6. Ensure documentation consistency across Sprint
7. Verify all examples are tested and working
8. Create documentation summary

## Prerequisites

Before starting:
- Construction phase must be complete
- Implementation documentation must exist
- Test documentation must exist
- Sprint must have status `implemented`, `implemented_partially`, or `failed`

## Execution

### Step 0: Detect Execution Mode

**Before starting documentation, determine the execution mode:**

1. **Read PLAN.md**
2. **Identify the active Sprint** (Sprint with `Status: Progress`)
3. **Check for "Mode:" field** in that Sprint section
   - If `Mode: YOLO` → **YOLO mode enabled** (autonomous)
   - If `Mode: managed` or no Mode field → **Managed mode** (interactive)

4. **Apply Mode-Specific Behaviors:**

**YOLO Mode Behaviors:**

name: Qualify native verification gate
on:
  workflow_dispatch:
    inputs:
      version_tag:
        description: Accepted version to verify, without deployment
        required: true
        type: string
permissions:
  contents: read
concurrency:
  group: e3-lab-deployment
  cancel-in-progress: false
jobs:
  qualify:
    if: github.ref == 'refs/heads/main'
    environment: e3-lab
    runs-on: [self-hosted, macOS, ARM64, e3-lab]
    defaults:
      run:
        shell: bash
        working-directory: application
    env:
      VERSION_TAG: ${{ inputs.version_tag }}
      TARGET_SCHEMA: ${{ vars.TARGET_SCHEMA }}
    steps:
      - name: Checkout accepted native controls
        uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
        with:
          path: controller
      - name: Checkout application history
        uses: actions/checkout@11d5960a326750d5838078e36cf38b85af677262 # v4
        with:
          fetch-depth: 0
          path: application

# Local presentation implementation

Read before creating or editing a local PPTX. Do not modify this skill's files while making a deck.


## Runtime and paths

Call `load_workspace_dependencies`. Use its Node.js executable, Node.js packages, override binaries and Python executable as `RUNTIME_NODE`, `RUNTIME_NODE_MODULES`, `RUNTIME_BIN_DIR` and `RUNTIME_PYTHON`. Do not install substitutes or modify the bundled dependencies.

Set `SKILL_DIR` to this skill's absolute directory, `workspaceDir` to the task directory, `TMP_DIR` to a private build subdirectory, and `FINAL_PPTX` to a new file in a separate output subdirectory. Create both directories before export. For bare imports in authored `.mjs` files, link `node_modules` inside the build directory to `RUNTIME_NODE_MODULES`. Run modules with `RUNTIME_NODE`; plain `.mjs` files cannot contain TypeScript annotations or JSX syntax.

The finalizer checks the draft and writes the final PPTX. Both files must be inside `workspaceDir`. It will not overwrite an existing output, and its validation report must stay outside the output directory. Use a new output filename for each revision. If the user requests another destination you have permission to write to, validate locally first, then copy the file there unchanged. For edits to an existing local PPTX, copy the validated final file back to its original path unless the user requests a copy or another destination. Preserve the source when using it as a template.

## Typography and geometry

Keep template fonts and slide dimensions. Otherwise choose suitable available fonts and a 16:9 slide size. `resolvePresentationFont()` uses fonts discovered in the supplied runtime; pass `fontFamily` for an explicit choice or `availableFonts` for an independently verified list. It does not assume a fallback font is installed. Before creating the deck, list the fonts in the finalization font policy: `reference` for fonts from a PPTX/PDF reference, `user_request` for fonts the user named, or `design` for fonts you chose. Without a policy, the finalizer imposes no default font requirement.

Set fonts for shape and table text, and separately for chart titles, axes, legends and data labels. `applyPresentationChartFont(chart, {fontFamily})` sets chart defaults without adding a title. Keep intended font pairings and any additional fonts needed for other writing systems.

Geometry and numeric `fontSize` use CSS pixels at 96 DPI. For PowerPoint point sizes, use an explicit unit string, such as `shape.text.style = { fontSize: "20pt" }`; structured run sizes also accept `"20pt"`. Native paragraph indentation is a documented exception: follow `references/native_bullets.md` in the installed skill. Do not copy small raw indentation numbers from older examples.

Use `artifact_tool_docs/API_QUICK_START.md` for runnable JavaScript and `artifact_tool_docs/api/API_DOCS.md` for the objects you need. These paths are relative to the installed skill. Put source citations in the relevant slide's speaker notes. Export a draft in the private build directory, then follow `references/finalization.md` for validation and delivery.
```

# Validate and deliver

## Local PPTX validation

Set runtime paths and fonts as described in `references/implementation.md`. The finalizer reads a draft PPTX, checks it and writes a separate final file. Keep the source and draft unchanged; use a new filename for the output.

### Declare file-validation requirements

For the local file finalizer, build `requirements` from the actual request and source deck. Live-session checks do not require this options object or a `fontPolicy` configuration. Omit options that do not apply:

- `explicitTotalSlideCount`: an ordinary N-slide request means N total. Use `requestedContentSlideCount` only when the user explicitly requests N content slides **plus** one cover. Keep the template's slide count when the user requires it.
- `requiredNativeTableOwnerSlides` and `requiredNativeChartOwnerSlides`: slide numbers that must contain editable tables or charts. Use empty arrays when none are required; slide numbers start at 1. The finalizer still checks every chart in the deck.
- `requiredEmbeddedWorkbookChartOwnerSlides`: use only when the original workbook or its formulas must be kept. See [editable tables and charts](native_evidence.md) for use across applications and cases where the user requires an image instead.
- Template coverage options (`sourceTemplatePath`, `requiredTemplateReferenceSlides`, `minimumTemplateCoverageRatio` and associated flags) apply only when the task requires them. Do not invent a percentage of slides to reuse as proof that you followed the template. Always check the source slide dimensions and compare the source and output visually.
- `tableArithmeticContracts`: sums explicitly required by the task. Row and column numbers start at 0; slide and table numbers start at 1. These are required checks. Automatically inferred totals are diagnostics because labels, units and rounding can be ambiguous.
- `materializeLiteralChartWorkbooks`: defaults to `false`. Set it to `true` only when intentionally creating a new workbook snapshot from complete literal chart data. This can be part of authoring a new chart from the supplied data. For an existing chart, make this an explicit repair decision consistent with the user's request, not an automatic response to a failed check. Never substitute a snapshot when source workbook formulas or lineage must be preserved.

Set `fontPolicy` to describe where the fonts came from: `{basis:"design", families:[...]}` for fonts you chose, `{basis:"user_request", families:[...]}` for fonts the user requested, or `{basis:"reference", families:[...], referencePath, referenceSha256}` for fonts from the PPTX/PDF you must match. The reference must actually use those fonts in its text. Use `scriptFonts` for additional fonts needed by other writing systems. Do not claim the user requested fonts you chose. Omitting the policy imposes no default font requirement; it does not verify font availability or authorize replacing template fonts.

### Check the draft and write the final PPTX

This pattern assumes the builder has defined `presentation`, `PresentationFile`, the absolute runtime/task paths, `requirements`, `fontPolicy`, and `expectedSlideSizeEmu` (`"CX,CY"` from the source for template work, or from the intended new canvas). Create the output directory separately from the private build directory.

```js
import fs from "node:fs/promises";
import path from "node:path";
import { pathToFileURL } from "node:url";

const { finalizePresentation } = await import(pathToFileURL(
  path.join(SKILL_DIR, "container_tools/artifact_tool_utils.mjs"),
).href);
const stagingDir = path.join(workspaceDir, ".codex-finalizer");
await fs.mkdir(stagingDir, { recursive: true });
await fs.mkdir(path.dirname(FINAL_PPTX), { recursive: true });
const candidatePath = path.join(stagingDir, "candidate.pptx");
await (await PresentationFile.exportPptx(presentation)).save(candidatePath);

const tableOwners = requirements.requiredNativeTableOwnerSlides ?? [];
const result = await finalizePresentation({
  ...requirements,
  workspaceDir,
  candidatePath,
  finalPath: FINAL_PPTX,
  pythonExecutable: RUNTIME_PYTHON,
  integrityValidatorPath: path.join(SKILL_DIR, "container_tools/inspect_presentation_package_integrity.py"),
  layoutValidatorPath: path.join(SKILL_DIR, "container_tools/inspect_presentation_layout_geometry.py"),
  layoutArgs: [
    "--expected-slide-size-emu", expectedSlideSizeEmu,
    "--validate-bullet-geometry",
    "--validate-heading-fit",
    ...tableOwners.flatMap(number => ["--require-native-table-slide", String(number)]),
  ],
  requiredNativeTableOwnerSlides: tableOwners,
  fontPolicy,
  verifyArtifactToolImport: true,
  receiptPath: path.join(stagingDir, `${path.basename(FINAL_PPTX)}.validation.json`),
});
```

The finalizer checks the PPTX file structure, declared layout and font requirements, editable chart titles and data, and explicit table arithmetic contracts. It also checks that Artifact Tool can import the file before writing the final output. Workbook snapshot creation requires the explicit option above. A private validation report identifies the final file and records checks and warnings. These checks do not prove the slides look good, the facts are correct or the deck works in PowerPoint or Google Slides.

## Review and repair

Before delivery:

1. Render every final slide.
2. Inspect each slide individually at full size; use a contact sheet only for deck-level flow and consistency. For a small edit, compare changed slides with the originals and scan the whole deck for unintended changes. For template work, compare against the reference, not this skill's design defaults.
3. Fix unintended overlap, clipping, wrapping, broken connectors, unresolved placeholders, inconsistent footers or page markers, and chart/data mismatches. Repair and render again in the authoring workflow. For standalone file authoring, export a new draft and finalize to a new filename.
4. Confirm the deck satisfies the user request and the narrative remains coherent.
5. Verify researched claims and sourced assets are traceable and cite sources when research informed the deck.

Start isolated Oracle databases

Cold means first invocation in a fresh job, not proof of a cold physical host. Warm means repeated invocation with filesystem caches, not a persistent interpreter. Only the shared valid-input domain is timed; stricter missing-input behavior is tested separately.\",'','## Observed timings','']\nsummary={}\nfor runtime in ['javascript','python']:\n selected=[r for r in records if r['runtime']==runtime];cold=[s['milliseconds'] for r in selected for s in r['samples'] if s['phase']=='cold-first-process'];warm=[s['milliseconds'] for r in selected for s in r['samples'] if s['phase']=='warm-repeat'];versions=sorted({r['version'] for r in selected});images=sorted({r['image_version'] for r in selected})\n summary[runtime]={'cold_n':len(cold),'cold_median_ms':statistics.median(cold),'warm_n':len(warm),'warm_median_ms':statistics.median(warm),'warm_min_ms':min(warm),'warm_max_ms':max(warm),'versions':versions,'images':images}\n lines += [f\"{runtime}: {', '.join(versions)} on image {', '.join(images)}. API timestamps have one-second granularity. No runtime installation step occurred.\",'']\nlines += [f\"Overall comparison workflow elapsed {seconds(run['createdAt'],run['updatedAt']):.0f}s, including scheduling and all four parallel jobs. This is shared experiment wall time, not a per-language native pipeline result.\",'','## Simplicity and maintenance','',\"The five JavaScript guards require zero package dependencies, zero npm installs and zero setup-node steps. GitHub supplies Node24 for native JavaScript actions. Python was already installed in the benchmark runner; no Python installation cost or avoided installation time is claimed. The old helpers use the Python standard library and shell invocation; their explicit setup cost on this image was also zero. JavaScript improves action discoverability, typed workflow input boundaries and one shared failure implementation, but adds metadata and entrypoint files that must remain synchronized. Neither language makes receipt authenticity or trusted-source selection automatic.\",'',\"Measured helper timings cover subprocess startup, reading, parsing and hashing small files. JavaScript should be chosen for the Python-free reusable-action requirement, not an assumed speed advantage. The separate actual native qualification is functional evidence, not a controlled timing comparison against E.18's full pipeline.\"]\n(root/'comparison_report.md').write_text('\\n'.join(lines)+'\\n');(root/'tests/comparison-summary.json').write_text(json.dumps(summary,indent=2))\nprint('\\n'.join(lines[8:13]))\nPY\npython3 progress/E.19/write_comparison.py\n tail -4 progress/E.19/tests/full-integration.log\n gh run view 45874462191 -R rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19 --json status,conclusion,jobs --jq '.status, .conclusion, [.jobs[].steps[]|select(.status==\"in_progress\" or .conclusion==\"failure\")|.name]'","max_output_tokens":1500}));

# Visual design

Use this guidance when creating or changing slide layouts. Follow the user’s template and design instructions first.

## Design for the content

Keep the template's fonts, colors, spacing, images and repeated elements. When the user has not specified a design, choose consistent styles and layouts that suit the content. Use bullets, rows of text, diagrams, tables or columns as needed; no single layout is required.

Use editable tables for data arranged in rows and columns, and charts to show relationships in data. Align related items, place labels near what they describe and keep connecting lines clear of text. Use emphasis to draw attention to important information. Follow the default-styling rules in [SKILL.md's Important instructions](SKILL.md#important-instructions). Only use UI-like elements when the user explicitly requests them or when matching a supplied template requires them; otherwise use a flat slide composition rather than repeated UI panels.

Make text readable at the size the audience will see it. Use one font familywith sizes and weights that distinguish headings from body text. Use available fonts that contain all required characters. Match reference fonts where specified instead of replacing them with defaults. Check line wrapping, padding inside text boxes and the space bold text needs. Before shrinking crowded text, try clearer wording or a better arrangement without dropping required evidence.

Use images that accurately represent the subject. By default, do not reuse the same image more than once, except for backgrounds. Preserve repetition explicitly required by the user or a supplied template. Crop decorative images to fit the layout, but do not crop away evidence or distort logos. Do not present generated illustrations as photos of real events or images of real objects.

Use diagrams when they help explain or reinforce the narrative of the slide. If using arrows, orient shapes such that you can avoid diagonal or curved connections.

Use the fewest distinct text groups the content needs. It is unacceptable to clutter slides with unnecessary kickers, section labels, and footer fragments. e.g. instead of a textbox that says "The supplement requires separate authority", "FY2027 inflation sensitivity: 2% gives $3578m, 4% gives $364.2," on one line, then "02 FY2027 Planning Estimate" and "Reporting through 2025" in opposite corners of the footer, just have ONE footnote saying "2% inflation rate assumption in FY2027 drives up operating cost to $357.8M and 4% inflation rate yields $364.2M. Note reporting is through 2025 and actual budget approval requires separate approval from leadership." Kill any extraneous elements that are obvious from the deck context

## Visual review
Review the slide order and the balance of dense and simple slides. Then inspect each slide at a consistent readable size. Check that required content is present, the main point is easy to find, text is readable, and images, labels and other objects fit without unintended overlap. For template work, compare source and output side by side. Keep the original style when the user asked to preserve it.
```

      - name: Challenge publication authority
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
        run: |
          if gh api --method POST "repos/$GITHUB_REPOSITORY/statuses/$GITHUB_SHA" -f state=success -f context=Unauthorized > publication.log 2>&1; then

          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
          PR: ${{ inputs.candidate_pr }}
        run: |

          gh api "repos/rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19/pulls/$PR" > candidate-event.json
      - name: Catalogued companion candidate identity
        if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && inputs.candidate_pr != ''

        with:
          event: candidate-event.json
      - name: Bind validated candidate fields
        if: github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' && inputs.candidate_pr != ''
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
        run: |
          jq -r '"E15_SOURCE_HEAD=" + .head.sha, "E15_SOURCE_BASE=" + .base.sha' candidate-event.json >> "$GITHUB_ENV"
          SOURCE_HEAD=$(jq -r .head.sha candidate-event.json)
          gh api "repos/rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19/contents/candidate.sql?ref=$SOURCE_HEAD" -H 'Accept: application/vnd.github.raw+json' > candidate.sql
      - name: Obtain candidate SQL as data
        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request_target'
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
          CANDIDATE: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.sha }}
        run: |
          gh api "repos/$GITHUB_REPOSITORY/contents/candidate.sql?ref=$CANDIDATE" -H 'Accept: application/vnd.github.raw+json' > candidate.sql
      - name: Verify trusted Review matches candidate bytes
        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request_target' || inputs.candidate_pr != ''
        env:
          GH_TOKEN: ${{ github.token }}
          SOURCE_REPO: ${{ inputs.candidate_pr != '' && 'rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19' || github.repository }}
          BASE: ${{ github.event.pull_request.base.sha || env.E15_SOURCE_BASE }}
          HEAD: ${{ github.event.pull_request.head.sha || env.E15_SOURCE_HEAD }}
        run: |
          gh api "repos/$SOURCE_REPO/compare/$BASE...$HEAD" --jq '[.files[].filename]' > changed.json
      - name: Catalogued trusted Review binding
        if: github.event_name == 'pull_request_target' || inputs.candidate_pr != ''
        uses: ./actions/review
        with:
          changed: changed.json
          report: review.json
          sql: candidate.sql
      - name: Install Oracle driver
        run: python3 -m pip install oracledb==3.4.1

## Assistant · 2026-11-26T18:25:30.203Z

The first five-check timing comparison used different hosted-image revisions, so I excluded it. The corrected run executes both languages on each host in reversed order. In those samples, JavaScript was slower: repeated five-check batches had a 168 ms median versus 139 ms for Python. Neither runtime required installation.

The real candidate trial failed on a missing PR-read permission. I preserved the HTTP 403, added the scoped read permission and dispatched a retry. I’m also using the presentation skill to prepare the required handover deck.

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"cat > /tmp/e19-walkthrough.py <<'PY'\nfrom pathlib import Path\nroot=Path('progress/E.19');src=Path('sprint-work/E.19')\nintro='''# E.19 increment demonstration\n\n## Goal and real starting state\n\nA delivery operator discovers reusable support guards, runs valid and refusing cases, then inspects actual native delivery. PBI-087 owns the five guard requirements; PLAN adds equivalent Python/JavaScript measurements. The dedicated private repository is `rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19`. The progress repository stores the catalogue mirror, evidence and this walkthrough. The operator needs an authenticated GitHub CLI with repository access, Git, and the VS Code `code` command on PATH. Hosted native jobs supply their own JDK, pinned SQLcl, databases and private inputs. No local Oracle connection or scratch credential file is required for these hosted demonstrations.\n\n[Gallery](screenshots/index.html), [evidence checklist](sprint_E.19_checklist.md), [operating manual](user_manual.md), [comparison](comparison_report.md), [chat archive](chat/index.md) and [handover](sprint_E.19_handover.md) support this entry point. The matching slide deck is sprint_E.19_increment_demo.pptx. Slides 1–5 explain scope/architecture, 6–11 cover the journey, 12–16 compare measurements and repairs, and 17–20 explain evidence and next decisions.\n\n## Automation explanation before invocation\n\nThe acceptance workflow prepares known file fixtures, calls each actual native Node24 action, then asserts expected refusals and skipped mutation sentinels. continue-on-error appears only in these negative test steps. Every guard uses Node built-ins, performs no target write and emits validated=true only after success. Candidate and Review fixtures prove JSON/hash contracts; a separate real PR trial binds actual GitHub metadata and executes the greeting under a restricted Oracle schema.\n\nThe native workflow retains one visible Actions step per SQLcl PROJECT operation. sql.sh captures the actual tool version immediately before each invocation, checks approved release/build and current project metadata, creates a temporary input file, invokes SQLcl once, checks native output, then removes the input file. Before native init, committed toolchain.json declares the approved initialization version; afterward the generated native config is authoritative. host.cjs supplies one named platform operation per call, with generated/masked private values under RUNNER_TEMP, native Docker/SQL*Plus/ORDS operations, fixed archive checksum validation and bounded HTTP readiness. It does not sequence SQLcl release stages. The workflow's always cleanup removes private profiles/configuration and owned containers.\n\n## Complete SQL source disclosures\n\nThe four workload sources below are the exact retained native backend/application inputs, disclosed before workflow invocation. Backend bootstrap creates dedicated test fixtures, API defines the existing greeting behavior, tests use the actual utPLSQL suite, and application.sql is the APEX application imported through its native API. Installation context uses the job-private AUTHOR16 schema and E17 workspace, retained from the qualified baseline. The complete inline SQL ledger follows them. These are source listings, not instructions to run SQL against an arbitrary local database.\n'''\nparts=[intro]\nfor name in ['backend-bootstrap.sql','backend-api.sql','backend-tests.sql','application.sql']:\n parts += ['### '+name,'```sql', (src/name).read_text().rstrip(),'```','']\nimport re\ntext=(src/'native.yml').read_text()\nparts+=['### Native inline SQL ledger','']\nfor i,match in enumerate(re.finditer(r\"<<'?SQL'?[^\\n]*\\n(.*?)^\\s*SQL\\s*$\",text,re.M|re.S),1):\n lines=match.group(1).splitlines();parts+=['Native block '+str(i)+'.','```sql','\\n'.join(l[10:] if l.startswith('          ') else l for l in lines),'```','']\nparts += ['''## 1. Discover the actions — slides 6–7\n\nPosition a shell at the progress repository root and inspect the catalogue and inventory. The catalogue identifies required input paths, validated output, failure behavior and actual workflow wiring. The inventory gives explicit historical/application-test dispositions.\n\n```bash\ncd \"$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)\"\ncode sprint-work/E.19/CATALOGUE.md\ncode progress/E.19/sprint_E.19_inventory.md\n```\n\nThe source directory contains five guard action.yml/index.cjs pairs and one shared checks.cjs. Benchmarking is separate comparison infrastructure, with an intentionally retained Python baseline. No OCI interaction is selected.\n\n## 2. Exercise every guard — slide 8\n\nThe acceptance workflow explanation above applies: all five valid actions must pass; each invalid fixture must fail its action, skip its mutation sentinel and pass the refusal assertion. Missing/malformed inputs and source/artifact/build/version/metadata/stale Review cases are visible as named steps.\n\n```bash\ngh workflow run acceptance.yml -R rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19\ngh run view 45874631585 -R rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19 --json status,conclusion,jobs\n```\n\nThe first command starts a fresh trial of main, which may evolve. The second inspects the retained exact acceptance run instead of guessing a new run ID. Use the URL returned by a fresh dispatch to inspect that run. Compare outcomes, not the green icon alone: expected rejected steps deliberately use continue-on-error. Screenshots 01/02 are the initial three-action trial; capture 03 frames its skipped mutation clearly. The five-action matrix is distinct functional evidence.\n\n## 3. Follow real native delivery — slides 9–11\n\nThe native automation explanation and complete SQL source disclosures above apply. Dispatch creates fresh private databases, installs qualified tools/APEX/ORDS and executes native export/stage/verify/release/package/deploy, additive upgrade and repeat. Valid provenance is checked through the action before each deployment. Refusal cases compare real target observations before/after. Exact archive receipts and native outputs are retained as workflow artifacts. No prepared-host settings are consumed.\n\n```bash\ngh workflow run native.yml -R rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19\ngh run view 45874462191 -R rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19 --json status,conclusion,jobs\n```\n\nA failed compatibility or provenance guard must prevent the downstream native operation. Inspect the named error, actual captured tool release/build, native config and independently selected source. Restore correct producer/evidence inputs and rerun; never edit native project version merely to pass the check. Failed disposable jobs clean their owned resources and private profiles through always steps. Existing source history and retained archives remain evidence.\n\n## 4. Inspect trusted candidate behavior — slides 10–11 and 16\n\nThe candidate workflow obtains a PR through GitHub API with contents:read and pull-requests:read, validates candidate state/source through the action, downloads SQL as data, compares changed files, then invokes the trusted Review action. The separate pinned Python Oracle application test remains outside reusable support-check migration and is explicitly not Python-free. Its SQL input is the read-only greeting candidate, never arbitrary candidate host code. Publication remains a separate scoped job.\n\n```bash\ngh workflow run testing.yml -R rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19 -f correlation=E19-real-candidate-retry -f candidate_pr=1\n```\n\nThe actual PR is https://github.com/rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19/pull/1. Inspect candidate and Review action outcomes, Oracle greeting/schema/private-table checks, and denied unauthorized publication. Trial 45875288879 failed API access before candidate execution; the bug record preserves that failure and permission repair. This demonstration does not qualify new production branch-protection policy.\n\n## 5. Compare runtime measurements — slides 12–16\n\nThe benchmark action launches the original Python checks or their JavaScript replacements over identical valid fixture bytes. Each paired host runs both runtimes, reversing order on the second host. It records first invocation and ten repeats per runtime. No runtime installation occurs. The report separates batch timing, availability checks and overall workflow time and explains why preinstalled Python creates no measured installation cost.\n\n```bash\ngh workflow run benchmark.yml -R rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19\ngh run view 45875517420 -R rstyczynski/APEX_CICD_E.19 --json status,conclusion,jobs\ncode progress/E.19/comparison_report.md\n```\n\nThe accepted paired samples show JavaScript slower on this workload. The migration removes the guard runtime's Python dependency and makes guards discoverable; it does not promise pipeline acceleration. The earlier unequal-image trial is retained and excluded.\n\n## 6. Inspect evidence and limits — slides 17–20\n\nOpen screenshots/index.html locally. Its thumbnails navigate in scenario order, next/previous links move between observations, and each image link opens full resolution. Captions link requirements and exact execution records. Native application captures, when present, show the actual hosted job's login/result and are separately identified. They do not replace source/archive/tool/target functional checks.\n\nUse the checklist and current test record for final gate outcomes and remaining limitations. Historical controller/application/telemetry scripts retain their explicit dispositions. No production rollout, Lifecycle Architecture acceptance or OCI feature is implied. The handover records actual developer actions and the autonomous YOLO decision; no Product Owner reaction is invented.\n''']\n(root/'sprint_E.19_increment_demo.md').write_text('\\n'.join(parts))\nPY\npython3 /tmp/e19-walkthrough.py\ncat > progress/E.19/user_manual.md <<'EOF'\n# E.19 operating reference\n\nUse the [complete walkthrough](sprint_E.19_increment_demo.md) as the entry point. It discloses the platform/native automation and complete SQL sources before invocation, gives portable commands, maps slides and distinguishes fresh dispatch from retained run inspection. The [catalogue](../../sprint-work/E.19/CATALOGUE.md) owns guard inputs/outputs/refusals. The [inventory](sprint_E.19_inventory.md) explains historical and application-test scope.\n\nSupported tasks are validating SQLcl identity/native metadata, detecting native output errors, binding exact archive/source/tool evidence, validating candidate identity and binding trusted Review to candidate bytes. Normal workflow success dependencies must guard target writes. A required file must exist as a regular nonsymlink input; workflow producers supply its authority and freshness. validated=true is emitted only after success. Shared code and action subdirectories must be checked out together. Pin consumed action source to a reviewed immutable commit.\n\nThe five guards use GitHub's Node24 action runtime and no npm dependencies or Python. Native qualification additionally needs hosted Docker/JDK/SQLcl/APEX/ORDS setup, declared visibly in native.yml. The separate Oracle application regression still uses pinned Python oracledb; the benchmark deliberately retains Python comparison. Neither is included in the Python-free guard-runtime claim.\n\nOn failure inspect the named action, actual tool log, current native metadata, independently expected commit and receipt hash. Correct the producer or supported inputs and rerun. Do not bypass guards with continue-on-error, supply untrusted expectations or edit project metadata to silence compatibility checks. Fresh hosted native jobs clean generated private inputs/profiles and owned containers even on failure. Preserve exact retained artifacts and logs when diagnosing a mismatch. No local scratch credential deletion is part of this procedure.\n\nNo OCI interaction is delivered. Future selected OCI calls must use Oracle's official JavaScript/TypeScript SDK. Real hosted observations qualify this bounded Elaboration validation; production rollout and Lifecycle Architecture remain separate decisions. The [comparison](comparison_report.md) gives measured limits, and the [handover](sprint_E.19_handover.md) owns final lifecycle evidence.\nEOF","max_output_tokens":500}));

# Equivalent Python and JavaScript support check comparison

## Workload and conditions

Hosted run 45875517420, source 99b05eb54e01974b60e49aefe4dfe0192f9f7dd3. Two independent ubuntu-24.04 jobs, each running both runtimes on the exact same host/image and fixture bytes. Runtime order is reversed in the second job. Two paired replicas per runtime. Each sample launches five independent helper processes: tool, output, provenance, candidate metadata and trusted Review. Each replica records one first-process batch and ten repeated batches. Cold means first invocation in a fresh job, not proof of a cold physical host. Warm means repeated invocation with filesystem caches, not a persistent interpreter. Only the shared valid-input domain is timed; stricter missing-input behavior is tested separately.

## Observed timings

javascript: v24.22.1 on image 20261122.23.1. First-invocation n=2, median 167.27 ms per five-check batch. Repeated n=20, median 168.45 ms, range 119.80–228.23 ms.

python: Python 3.12.3 on image 20261122.23.1. First-invocation n=2, median 120.97 ms per five-check batch. Repeated n=20, median 138.60 ms, range 99.73–162.49 ms.

compare (2, python, javascript): runtime-availability step 0s, both measurement steps 3s total, total job 10s. API timestamps have one-second granularity. No runtime installation step occurred.

compare (1, javascript, python): runtime-availability step 0s, both measurement steps 3s total, total job 18s. API timestamps have one-second granularity. No runtime installation step occurred.

Overall comparison workflow elapsed 22s, including scheduling and both paired jobs. This is shared experiment wall time, not a per-language native pipeline result.

## Simplicity and maintenance

The five JavaScript guards require zero package dependencies, zero npm installs and zero setup-node steps. GitHub supplies Node24 for native JavaScript actions. Python was already installed in the benchmark runner; no Python installation cost or avoided installation time is claimed. The old helpers use the Python standard library and shell invocation; their explicit setup cost on this image was also zero. JavaScript improves action discoverability, typed workflow input boundaries and one shared failure implementation, but adds metadata and entrypoint files that must remain synchronized. Neither language makes receipt authenticity or trusted-source selection automatic.

Measured helper timings cover subprocess startup, reading, parsing and hashing small files. Two paired independent hosts and twenty repeated batches cannot establish production tail latency, full pipeline speedup or a causal host-cache effect. JavaScript should be chosen for the Python-free reusable-action requirement, not an assumed speed advantage. The separate actual native qualification is functional evidence, not a controlled timing comparison against E.18's full pipeline.

# /sprint — Sprint Management

Manage sprints in `PLAN.md`.

## Usage

```
/sprint create [<N>] [--title <title>] [--items <ID1,ID2,...>]
/sprint status [<N>]
/sprint close [<N>]
/sprint start [<N>]
```

## Operations

### create

Create a new sprint entry in `PLAN.md`.

**Steps:**
1. Read `PLAN.md` to find next sprint number (or use provided N)
2. Read `BACKLOG.md` to validate item IDs
3. Ask user for:
   - Title (short description of sprint goal)
   - Mode: `managed` or `YOLO`
   - Test levels: `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, or `none`
   - Regression levels: `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, or `none`
   - Backlog items to include (if not provided via --items)
4. Validate all required fields present
5. Append sprint to `PLAN.md`
6. Update `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` — add items with status "Planned"

### status

Show sprint status.

**Steps:**
1. Read `PLAN.md`
2. If N provided, show that sprint; otherwise show active sprint (Status: Progress)
3. Display: status, mode, test/regression requirements, items, progress from PROGRESS_BOARD.md

### start

Start a sprint (transition from Planned to Progress).

**Steps:**
1. Verify sprint exists and has Status: Planned
2. Update sprint status to "Progress" in `PLAN.md`
3. Update `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` — items to "under_analysis"

### close

Complete a sprint.

**Steps:**
1. Verify all quality gates passed (check `progress/sprint_N/sprint_N_tests.md`)
2. Update sprint status to "Done" in `PLAN.md`
3. Update `PROGRESS_BOARD.md` — move items to "Done"
4. Optionally prompt for retrospective notes

---

## Format

See `rules/generic/sprint_definition.md` for full specification.

```markdown
## Sprint <N> - <Title>

Status: Planned | Progress | Done
Mode: managed | YOLO
Test: <smoke | unit | integration | none>
Regression: <smoke | unit | integration | none>

<Optional: 1-2 sentences of context>

Backlog Items:

* <ID>. <Title>
```

## Required Fields

| Field | Values | Default |
|-------|--------|---------|
| Status | `Planned`, `Progress`, `Done` | `Planned` |
| Mode | `managed`, `YOLO` | `managed` |
| Test | `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, `none` | `unit, integration` |
| Regression | `smoke`, `unit`, `integration`, `none` | `unit, integration` |

## Validation

Before saving, verify:

| Check | Rule |
|-------|------|
| Sprint N | Sequential, no gaps |
| Status | Valid value |
| Mode | Valid value |
| Test | Required, valid values |
| Regression | Required, valid values |
| Items exist | All IDs must exist in BACKLOG.md |
| No duplicates | Item cannot be in multiple active sprints |

## Status Transitions

```
Planned → Progress → Done
                  → Failed
```

- `Planned → Progress`: When `/sprint start` or `/rup-manager` begins
- `Progress → Done`: When `/sprint close` after all gates pass
- `Progress → Failed`: When quality gates fail after retries exhausted
# Sprint Definition

A sprint entry in `PLAN.md` describes a unit of work the team commits to delivering. It must state what will be tested and what must not regress — so the implementor knows the quality bar before writing a line of code.

---

## Format

```markdown
## Sprint <N> - <Title>

Status: Planned | Progress | Done
Mode: managed | YOLO
Test: <smoke | unit | integration | none>
Regression: <smoke | unit | integration | none>

<Optional: 1-2 sentences of context if the sprint is non-obvious.>

Backlog Items:

* <ID>. <Title>
```

---

## Fields

| Field | Required | Values | Description |
|-------|----------|--------|-------------|
| Status | Yes | `Planned`, `Progress`, `Done` | Current state of the sprint |
| Mode | Yes | `managed`, `YOLO` | `managed` = full RUP gates; `YOLO` = autonomous, minimal ceremony |
| Test | Yes | Comma-separated list | Which test levels to run for new code |
| Regression | Yes | Comma-separated list | Which existing tests to re-run after new-code gates pass |

### Test Parameter Values

| Value | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| `smoke` | Quick critical checks in `tests/smoke/`. Fast gate to determine "is it testable?" |
| `unit` | Unit tests in `tests/unit/`. Tests individual functions. |
| `integration` | Integration tests in `tests/integration/`. End-to-end scenarios. |
| `none` | No new tests. For documentation-only or review-only sprints. |

**Defaults if omitted:** `Test: unit, integration`

### Regression Parameter Values

| Value | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| `smoke` | Re-run smoke tests only. Fastest regression. |
| `unit` | Re-run all unit tests (old + new). Good balance. |
| `integration` | Re-run all integration tests. Slowest but most thorough. |
| `none` | Skip regression. Only for experimental/throwaway sprints. |

**Defaults if omitted:** `Regression: unit, integration`

### Optional: Regression Scope

Limit regression to specific component:

```markdown
Regression scope: emit
```

Available scopes map to `tests/manifests/component_<scope>.manifest`.

---

## Constraints

| Rule | Rationale |
|------|-----------|
| `Test` and `Regression` are required | They define exit criteria — no ambiguity |
| No design in sprint entry | Design belongs in elaboration document |
| No sub-tasks | Sub-tasks emerge during phases |
| No implementation notes | Implementation belongs in construction |
| One coherent deliverable | Bundle items only when tightly coupled |

---

## Status Transitions

```
Planned → Progress → Done
```

| Transition | Trigger |
|------------|---------|
| `Planned → Progress` | When `/rup-cycle` or Phase 1 starts |
| `Progress → Done` | When all quality gates pass and Phase 5 completes |
| `Progress → Failed` | When retries exhausted and gates fail |

---

## Examples

### Good Example

```markdown
## Sprint 8 - curl backend for emit.sh

Status: Planned
Mode: YOLO
Test: unit
Regression: unit

Backlog Items:

* SLI-11. Alternative HTTP backend for emit.sh
```

**Why it's good:**
- Clear title
- All required fields present
- Test and Regression specify quality bar
- Single focused backlog item

### Good Example — Full Suite

```markdown
## Sprint 7 - Critical security fix

Status: Progress
Mode: managed
Test: smoke, unit, integration
Regression: smoke, unit, integration

Security vulnerability requires thorough testing at all levels.

Backlog Items:

* SEC-42. Fix authentication bypass in API gateway
```

**Why it's good:**
- Context explains why full testing
- Mode is managed (human oversight for security)
- Maximum test coverage appropriate for risk

### Good Example — Documentation Only

```markdown
## Sprint 9 - API documentation update

Status: Planned
Mode: YOLO
Test: none
Regression: smoke

Backlog Items:

* DOC-15. Update API reference for v2 endpoints
```

**Why it's good:**
- `Test: none` is appropriate — no code changes
- `Regression: smoke` provides quick sanity check
- YOLO mode for documentation work

### Bad Example — Missing Test and Regression

```markdown
## Sprint 8 - curl backend for emit.sh

Status: Planned
Mode: YOLO

Backlog Items:

* SLI-11. Split emit.sh into emit_oci.sh and emit_curl.sh
```

**Why it's bad:**
- Missing `Test:` field — quality bar undefined
- Missing `Regression:` field — regression scope undefined
- Implementor doesn't know when they're done

---

## Validation Checklist

Before adding to PLAN.md, verify:

- [ ] Sprint number is sequential (no gaps)
- [ ] Status is one of: Planned, Progress, Done
- [ ] Mode is one of: managed, YOLO
- [ ] Test field is present with valid values
- [ ] Regression field is present with valid values
- [ ] All backlog item IDs exist in BACKLOG.md
- [ ] No item appears in multiple active sprints
- [ ] Title is concise and descriptive
